 // Energy transfer coefficient\n    val_t external_flow;   // External energy source/sink\n};\n\n// Static connectivity information for each element\nstruct ElementStatic {\n    idx_t material_idx;\n    idx_t num_connections;\n    idx_t connected_idx[MAX_CONNECTIONS];     // Indices of connected elements\n    val_t connected_flux[MAX_CONNECTIONS];    // Flux coefficients for each connection\n};\n\n// Dynamic state for each element\nstruct ElementDynamic {\n    val_t current_energy;\n    val_t total_flux;\n};\n\n// Allocator that leaves trivially-constructible elements uninitialized on resize().\n// This keeps the pages untouched until the parallel initialization loop runs, so that\n// first-touch places each page on the NUMA node of the thread that will work on it.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct DefaultInitAllocator : std::allocator<T> {\n    using std::allocator<T>::allocator;\n\n    template <typename U>\n    struct rebind {\n        using other = DefaultInitAllocator<U>;\n    };\n\n    template <typename U, typename...  // Energy transfer coefficient\n    val_t external_flow;   // External energy source/sink\n};\n\n// Static connectivity information for each element\nstruct ElementStatic {\n    idx_t material_idx;\n    idx_t num_connections;\n    idx_t connected_idx[MAX_CONNECTIONS];     // Indices of connected elements\n    val_t connected_flux[MAX_CONNECTIONS];    // Flux coefficients for each connection\n};\n\n// Dynamic state for each element\nstruct ElementDynamic {\n    val_t current_energy;\n    val_t total_flux;\n};\n\n// Allocator that leaves trivially-constructible elements uninitialized on resize().\n// This keeps the pages untouched until the parallel initialization loop runs, so that\n// first-touch places each page on the NUMA node of the thread that will work on it.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct DefaultInitAllocator : std::allocator<T> {\n    using std::allocator<T>::allocator;\n\n    template <typename U>\n    struct rebind {\n        using other = DefaultInitAllocator<U>;\n    };\n\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U* p, Args&&... args) {\n        if constexpr (sizeof...(Args) == 0) {\n            ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n        } else {\n            ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U(std::forward<Args>(args)...);\n        }\n    }\n};\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nusing numa_vector = std::vector<T, DefaultInitAllocator<T>>;\n\n// World state\nstruct World {\n    std::vector<Material> materials;\n    numa_vector<ElementStatic> elements_static;\n    numa_vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic;\n    numa_vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize elements and build connectivity in parallel. Each element writes only\n    // its own entries, so the loop is trivially parallel; the static schedule matches the\n    // one used by the simulation kernel, giving NUMA-local first touch of every page.\n    ElementStatic* const es = world.elements_static.data();\n    ElementDynamic* const ed = world.elements_dynamic.data();\n    ElementDynamic* const eds = world.elements_dynamic_swap.data();\n\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (int idx = 0; idx < n_elems; ++idx) {\n        {\n            const int x = idx / n_elems_root;\n            const int y = idx % n_elems_root;\n            ElementStatic& elem = es[idx];\n\n            elem.material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n            elem.num_connections = 0;\n            ed[idx].current_energy = 0.0;\n            ed[idx].total_flux = 0.0;\n            eds[idx].current_energy = 0.0;\n            eds[idx].total_flux = 0.0;\n\n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n\n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n\n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Leave the unused connection slots deterministic\n            for (idx_t n = elem.num_connections; n < Args>\n    void construct(U* p, Args&&... args) {\n        if constexpr (sizeof...(Args) == 0) {\n            ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n        } else {\n            ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U(std::forward<Args>(args)...);\n        }\n    }\n};\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nusing numa_vector = std::vector<T, DefaultInitAllocator<T>>;\n\n// World state\nstruct World {\n    std::vector<Material> materials;\n    numa_vector<ElementStatic> elements_static;\n    numa_vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic;\n    numa_vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize elements and build connectivity in parallel. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CegoNYBxrTfwd6E4qXdV9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Parallelized with OpenMP; output is bit-identical to the original serial code.\n\n## Changes — `unstructured/unstructured.cpp`\n\n**Kernel (`runSimulation`)** — one `#pragma omp parallel` region spans the whole iteration loop instead of a fork/join per iteration. Inside, `#pragma omp for schedule(static) nowait` + an explicit `barrier` gives exactly one barrier per iteration. The buffer swap is replaced by parity-selected read/write pointers (`buffers[iter & 1]`), with a single `std::swap` after the region when `n_iters` is odd, so the final data lands in `elements_dynamic` exactly as before.\n\n**NUMA first touch** — this is the big win on this dual-socket EPYC. `std::vector::resize` value-initializes on the master thread, which would place every page on one NUMA node. A `DefaultInitAllocator` (default-init instead of value-init for the trivially-constructible element structs) leaves pages untouched, and mesh construction was rewritten as a flat `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` loop over element index — the same distribution the kernel uses — so each thread first-touches the pages it later reads. Unused connection slots are explicitly zeroed to keep the uninitialized memory deterministic.\n\n**`computeHash`** — `reduction(^:hash)`; XOR is associative/commutative so it's bit-exact.\n\n`validateResults` stays serial: a parallel FP sum would change the low bits of the printed energy/flux sums, and it's untimed.\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`** — `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` + link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. Executable name unchanged, no new files or dependencies.\n\n## Results (4096², 100 iters)\n\n| Threads | Time | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 15832 ms | 1.0× |\n| 8 | 2438 ms | 6.5× |\n| 32 | 1278 ms | 12.4× |\n| 64 | 1178 ms | 13.4× |\n| 128 | 1172 ms | 13.5× |\n| 256 (SMT) | 1349 ms | 11.7× |\n\nScaling saturates near 32–64 threads because the kernel is memory-bandwidth bound: `ElementStatic` is 144 B/element (fixed `MAX_CONNECTIONS=8` slots, only 4 used), so ~152 B moves per element-update versus ~22 FLOPs. At 128 threads that's ~220 GB/s of the machine's achievable bandwidth. Compressing the connectivity layout would raise the ceiling substantially, but it changes the mesh data structure and the reported memory-usage figures, so I left it alone — say the word if you want that variant.\n\nVerified bit-identical hashes, `-v` validation output and `-r` element dumps against a reference build of the original serial code for `-n 1/2/7/33/300/512/1024` with odd, even and zero iteration counts, at thread counts 1–256.\n\nOne thing to flag: `unstructured/build/` contained a CMake cache from a different machine (`/home/petert/...`) and could not build in place — that was pre-existing, unrelated to these changes. I regenerated it, so the tree now builds with a plain `cmake -S . -B build && cmake --build build`."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":854,"cache_read_input_tokens":44055,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":854},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cbe91d5a-8891-4386-a957-9626532eb567","uuid":"4c9ec369-53df-4382-9f20-33853e6c578a","timestamp":"2026-09-03T16:12:29.378Z","request_id":"req_011CegoNXMcSUDgxFndv2f6o"}
